--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes \( O \) and \( S \), we need to analyze the paths between these nodes.

1. **Identify the black nodes:** \( O \) and \( S \).
2. **Identify the immediate neighbors of \( O \):** \( N \), \( C \), \( B \).
3. **Identify the immediate neighbors of \( S \):** \( F \), \( E \), \( A \).
4. **Find all paths between \( O \) and \( S \):**
   - \( O \to N \to B \to E \to S \)
   - \( O \to N \to B \to F \to S \)
   - \( O \to N \to B \to A \to S \)
   - \( O \to C \to G \to T \to F \to S \)
   - \( O \to C \to G \to T \to E \to S \)
   - \( O \to C \to G \to T \to A \to S \)
   - \( O \to B \to E \to S \)
   - \( O \to B \to F \to S \)
   - \( O \to B \to A \to S \)

5. **Determine the minimum cut set:**
   - To disconnect \( O \) and \( S \), we need to remove edges that cover all paths.
   - By removing \( (O, N) \) and \( (O, C) \), we cut off the paths through \( N \) and \( C \).
   - Additionally, removing \( (B, E) \) will cut off the direct path through \( B \) to \( S \).

Therefore, the minimum number of edges to remove is 3.

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(O, N), (O, C), (B, E)].
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': [['N', 'O'], ['S', 'O']]}

To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (R and H) in the graph, we need to explore the paths connecting these two nodes and find the bottleneck nodes that, when removed, will break all paths between R and H.

1. **Identify the black nodes**: Node R (top-left, black-filled) and Node H (bottom-right, black-filled).
2. **Examine the paths between R and H**:
   - R is directly connected to F.
   - F is connected to E, B, and C.
   - E is connected to S and B.
   - B is a central node, connected to E, S, T, A, N, and F.
   - S is connected to Q and A.
   - A is connected to P and I.
   - P is connected to I and Q.
   - Q is connected to J.
   - J is connected to I.
   - I is connected to N.
   - N is connected to T and O.
   - T is connected to C, B, and G.
   - O is connected to G and C.
   - G is connected to C.
   - C is also connected to R.
   - This fully forms the complex connections.

3. **Evaluate the paths**:
   - The paths connecting R and H can be broken down:
     - R → F → B → N → H
     - R → F → B → T → O → G → C → T → N → H
     - R → C → T → N → H
     - R → C → O → N → H
     - R → F → B → T → N → H
   - By analyzing these paths, we identify bottleneck nodes that, if removed, will break multiple paths.

4. **Determine the minimum cut set**:
   - Removing F breaks the path R → F → B → N → H and many related paths.
   - Removing B breaks multiple paths involving B.
   - Removing N directly disconnections the remaining paths to H.

5. **Check the answer**:
   - If F, B, and N are removed:
     - All paths between R and H are disconnected.
     - No other single node can disconnect R and H on its own without including paths through other intermediary nodes.

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [F, B, N]

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the Black Nodes:**
   - The black nodes are \( P \) and \( R \).

2. **Identify the Neighbors of Each Black Node:**
   - Node \( P \) is directly connected to nodes: \( R \), \( O \), and \( Q \).
   - Node \( R \) is directly connected to nodes: \( P \), \( O \), and \( Q \).

3. **Find All Paths Between \( P \) and \( R \):**
   - Direct path: \( (P, R) \).
   - Indirect paths through \( O \): \( (P, O, R) \).
   - Indirect paths through \( Q \): \( (P, Q, R) \).

4. **Determine the Minimum Cut Set:**
   - To disconnect \( P \) and \( R \), we need to remove all paths between them.
   - Removing the direct edge \( (P, R) \) does not suffice because of the indirect paths through \( O \) and \( Q \).
   - Removing edges \( (P, O) \), \( (P, Q) \), and \( (R, Q) \) will disconnect \( P \) and \( R \) completely.

### Cut Set:
- The minimum cut set that disconnects \( P \) and \( R \) is: \( (P, R) \), \( (P, O) \), and \( (P, Q) \).

### Output Format:
Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(P, R), (P, O), (P, Q)].

To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (A and H), we need to analyze the paths between them.

1. **Identify the paths from node A to node H:**
   - A -> B -> G -> H
   - A -> B -> C -> H
   - A -> E -> B -> C -> H
   - A -> E -> B -> G -> H
   - A -> F -> B -> G -> H
   - A -> F -> B -> C -> H
   - A -> F -> T -> G -> H
   - A -> S -> B -> G -> H
   - A -> S -> B -> C -> H

2. **Determine the critical edges:**
   - The paths from A to H involve intermediate nodes B, C, and G. Specifically, the edges (B, C), (B, G), and (G, H) are part of multiple paths.

3. **Removal of edges to disconnect A and H:**
   - Removing edge (B, C): This eliminates the paths involving B -> C -> H.
   - Removing edge (B, G): This eliminates the paths involving B -> G -> H.
   - Removing edge (G, H): This eliminates the direct path G -> H.

By removing these three edges, all paths between A and H are disrupted.

**Answer:**
Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(B, C), (B, G), (G, H)].

2. **Identify the immediate neighboring nodes of H:**
   - H is connected to G and T.

3. **Find connections from E's neighbors to H's neighbors:**
   - B is not connected to G or T.
   - F is not connected to G or T.
   - S is connected to T.
   - N is connected to T.

From the above observations:
- The path from E to H through S:
  - E -> S -> T -> H results in a path length of 3.
- The path from E to H through N:
  - E -> N -> T -> H also results in a path length of 3.

We need to verify if there is a shorter path:
- By extending the search, we find:
  - S is connected to T (as observed), but there is no shorter alternative path through B, F, or other connections.

After evaluating all paths:
- The shortest path found is through S or N, both leading to the path length of 3.

**Path through S:**
- E -> S -> T -> H

**Path through N:**
- E -> N -> T -> H

Therefore, the shortest path length between E and H is:

**Length: 3, Path: [E, S, T, H]**
